# Data attribution and rights

## Botanical safety checker (v0.8, 2026-11-12)

- **NIH NCCIH fact sheets.** `frontend/demo/safety-knowledge.json` quotes sentences verbatim from 25 National Center for Complementary and Integrative Health "Usefulness and Safety" pages (https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/…), retrieved 2026-11-12. Each page states: "This publication is not copyrighted and is in the public domain." Page URLs, "Last Updated" dates and SHA-256 of the page text are stored per source; `scripts/build_safety_knowledge.py` refuses to write any quote it cannot find word-for-word. Quoting NIH does not imply NIH review or endorsement of FoliaTrace.
- **NIH Dietary Supplement Label Database (DSLD).** Live label search and label records are requested in the browser from `api.ods.od.nih.gov` (U.S. Government work). Only the search words or a label ID are sent. `reports/label_matching_*` cache only derived counts; cached raw records stay in the ignored `.runtime/` folder.
- **Statistics on the homepage.** CDC NCHS Data Brief No. 399 (2021): 57.6% of U.S. adults aged 20+ used any dietary supplement in the past 30 days (2017–2018). Mehta DH, Gardiner PM, Phillips RS, McCarthy EP. *J Altern Complement Med* 2008;14(10):1263–1269: only 33% of herb and dietary supplement users reported disclosure to a conventional provider (NHIS 2002).
- **Medicine names.** Generic and brand names in the medicine list are factual identifiers curated by the project; brand names belong to their owners.
- **Third-party browser code bundled in the public build:** tesseract.js 5.1.1 and tesseract.js-core (Apache-2.0); English LSTM model data from `@tesseract.js-data/eng` 1.0.0 (package MIT; Tesseract tessdata, Apache-2.0); qrcode-generator 1.4.4 by Kazuhiko Arase (MIT; "QR Code" is a registered trademark of DENSO WAVE). onnxruntime-web and @huggingface/tokenizers as listed in `data/reranker.lock.json`.
- **Example people and labels** in `frontend/demo/safety-examples.js` are fictional and do not describe any real product or brand.

DSH V2 handoffs (2026-11-05): source text, selected studies, researcher decisions and original rights markers may travel in a local handoff export. Export does not grant redistribution rights. In particular, locally acquired PubMed abstracts remain publisher-owned and are not added to the public source package. Imported handoffs are unverified external records, not new source acquisitions or scientific validation. Existing data snapshots, licences and original evaluation records are preserved.

Code MIT does not relicense these records. Only the selected records and allowed fields are bundled. Article licence versions are not supplied by the Europe PMC index; follow the original article licence statement. No figures, full modern articles, restricted traditional knowledge or modern Gutenberg editorial notes are bundled.

## Selected licensed Methods and abstract Results paragraphs (2026-10-21)

On 2026-10-23, four Methods paragraphs from the **Skin Biophysical Parameters** section of Szymczak K, Krajewska A, Grzyb M, Jodłowska I, Mietlińska K and Bonikowski R, **Upcycling Roman Chamomile Hydrolate and Apple Pomace Agri-Wastes into Sustainable Cosmetic Ingredients**, [PMC13024467](https://europepmc.org/articles/PMC13024467), DOI [10.3390/antiox15030380](https://doi.org/10.3390/antiox15030380), were added. The original JATS explicitly grants [CC BY 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/). The selected paragraphs describe ten female participants, eligibility, a 14-day fixed-side comparison of a base emulsion with the combined hydrolate/apple-extract emulsion, and skin measurements. Fixed sides are not randomized allocation. The paper combines laboratory work with a split-face study; neither its ingredient-specific clinical effect nor a historical remedy's efficacy follows from that design. Section anchors, authorship, normalized whitespace and document/excerpt hashes are retained. No additional Results excerpt, images or full article are redistributed.

The supplement also includes 25 Methods paragraphs, one referenced formulation table (Table 8), and the explicitly numbered abstract Results segment from Lile IE, Samoilă AOJ, Cojocariu C, Tapalagă G, Oláh NK, Stana OL, Berari A and Marian D, **Comparative Clinical Evaluation of Chamomile, Sage, and Ginger Mouthwashes in Reducing Plaque and Gingival Inflammation**, [PMC13203699](https://europepmc.org/articles/PMC13203699). Its original JATS explicitly grants [CC BY 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/). Table cells are transcribed with `|` separators and identified as a table transcription; offsets refer to that stored representation. Results are copied only between explicit numbered Results and Discussion headings; no missing result was generated. The table distinguishes three herbal arms; it is not evidence that the three plants were administered as one mixture. Section/table anchors, document/excerpt hashes and the original author attribution are retained. No images or full article are redistributed.

`data/demo/study_methods.json` adds 12 Methods paragraphs and one explicitly headed abstract Results paragraph from Linas I, Vavricka SR, Langhorst J and Pohl D, **Treatment of disorders of gut–brain interaction with peppermint oil and caraway oil combination Menthacarin: A phase IV clinical trial**, DOI [10.1007/s10354-025-01119-2](https://doi.org/10.1007/s10354-025-01119-2), [PMC13013221](https://europepmc.org/articles/PMC13013221). The original JATS explicitly licenses the article under [CC BY 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/). Paragraphs have normalized whitespace, section paths, source links and hashes. Results are the authors' report about their own intervention and population, not an inferred historical-remedy effect or a newly assigned condition-specific direction. No images or full article are redistributed. Original licence and author attribution apply to these excerpts; the code licence does not replace them. `scripts/acquire_study_methods.py` acquires this bounded, attributable supplement through Europe PMC. This is selected full-text context, not systematic full-text coverage.

## Selected controlled-trial factual reading notes

`data/reading_notes/peppermint_ibs.json` and `data/demo/trial_readings.json` contain three explicitly Codex AI-assisted factual readings of original clinical-study abstracts, with bibliographic metadata retrieved from Europe PMC and links to [Cash et al., PMID 26319955](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26319955/), [Weerts et al., PMID 31470006](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31470006/), and [Nee et al., PMID 34319275](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/34319275/). Original authors, DOI, metadata provenance and source rights indicators are retained. Source abstracts/full texts are not redistributed in this reading set and are not added to the 415 reusable-abstract collection. The project's own factual notes are dedicated under CC0-1.0; this does not relicense article prose. This is a purposive comparison for reading different formulations and endpoints, not expert gold, automatic extraction, risk-of-bias appraisal or a systematic review. `scripts/acquire_trial_readings.py` reattaches verified identifiers and bibliographic metadata to the declared note source.

## Historical corpus and abstract records

Historical source: Nicholas Culpeper, The Complete Herbal, Thomas Kelly, London, MDCCCL (1850). Gutenberg ebook 49513, transcription by Chris Curnow, Emmy and Distributed Proofreaders, source images Internet Archive. Only historical prose sections are included; images/modern notes excluded. Whitespace is normalized. Edition context and original attribution remain visible.

External clinical annotations: Eric Lehman, Jay DeYoung, Regina Barzilay, Byron C. Wallace. Evidence Inference (NAACL 2019; expanded BioNLP 2020). Annotation repository MIT; its full copyright/permission notice is included in data/licenses/evidence-inference-MIT.txt. Original article support spans separately CC BY/CC0. The project did not conduct this human annotation. Full raw CSV files are not redistributed.

## Independent records and local name lists (2026-11-02)

`data/demo/duke_ethnobotany.json` holds 77,621 of the 82,873 records in the ETHNOBOT table of Duke JA, *Dr. Duke's Phytochemical and Ethnobotanical Databases*, USDA Agricultural Research Service, 2016 ([doi:10.15482/USDA.ADC/1239279](https://doi.org/10.15482/USDA.ADC/1239279)), CC0 1.0. Activity, place, local name and citation stay as Duke published them. Only the GBIF accepted species was added.

`data/demo/merrill_1903_names.json` holds 2,846 local names from Merrill ED, *A Dictionary of the Plant Names of the Philippine Islands*, Bureau of Government Laboratories Bulletin 8, Manila, 1903, public domain, read from the [Internet Archive scan](https://archive.org/details/adictionaryplan00merrgoog). The text is OCR and was cleaned by rule, so a name is a hypothesis to check against the passage.

These names and uses belong to the communities that gave them. The lists are colonial-era and scholarly records of that knowledge, often without naming who shared it. FoliaTrace shows the source of each name and never presents a recorded use as a treatment.

`data/demo/duke_chemistry.json` joins the FARMACY and AGGREGAC tables of the same Duke database (CC0): for 1,459 species, the rarest chemicals with a recorded activity on each health topic.

`data/demo/ema_herbal.json` records, for 106 EU herbal monographs, the indication wording and whether the Committee on Herbal Medicinal Products filed it as well-established or traditional use. Source: European Medicines Agency, [herbal medicines table](https://www.ema.europa.eu/en/medicines/download-medicine-data) and the linked monographs, reproduced with acknowledgement of the source. The monographs remain the authoritative text.

`data/demo/local_names_wikidata.json` holds 23,793 plant names from Wikidata labels (CC0) in 42 languages and 282 Hausa names from Dalziel JM, *A Hausa Botanical Vocabulary*, 1916, public domain, Project Gutenberg 74245.

`data/demo/more_language_names.json` holds 10,022 Wikidata labels and aliases (CC0) in Polish, Czech, Dutch, Slovenian, Hungarian, Ukrainian, Macedonian, Slovak and Bosnian/Serbo-Croatian for 1,712 species already in the gazetteer.

`data/demo/field_study_names.json` holds local plant names read from the tables of 26 open-access field studies (Europe PMC). Each name is a fact recorded by the study and links to it; no table, text or figure is reproduced. The names belong to the communities who shared them with the researchers. # NIH Dietary Supplement Label Database reference records (2026-11-05)

Current lookup uses bounded pages with an explicit offset, market filter and sort. Users may request another page; the reference set still contains only the six frozen examples. New runtime label snapshots carry `NUMERIC_JSON_V1` to make exact safe numbers portable across browser JSON. This does not alter the frozen raw files or their original manifest hashes; legacy packets retain their original hash representation.

`data/product_labels/` contains six explicitly bounded US label JSON records retrieved from the official NIH ODS DSLD v9 API. Each identifier, acquisition URL, canonical SHA256 and acquisition date is in `manifest.json`. The official [API documentation](https://api.ods.od.nih.gov/dsld/v9/) identifies its data license as CC0 1.0. Package images, logos and label PDFs are referenced by original URL in metadata but are not downloaded or bundled. Product/brand names remain their owners' marks. Label text records manufacturer declarations, not an NIH endorsement, verification of contents, recommended patient use, proof of efficacy or current availability. Off-market records and formulation changes are preserved; these six examples are not a current or representative market database. Live lookup is optional, bounded to ten search hits, and sends only the separate product query to NIH. Other medicines, allergy details, reported use and visit labels are not part of those requests. Source snapshots do not authenticate the actual bottle or an imported record's author.

# Optional question-specific Europe PMC retrieval (2026-11-05)

Professional question review can optionally request PubMed-indexed records through the official [Europe PMC REST service](https://dev.europepmc.org/RestfulWebService). This is a separate question-specific retrieval, not an update of the redistributed corpus. Bibliographic and abstract source URLs, retrieval dates and response hashes are retained locally. Publisher rights to abstracts remain in force; this change does not grant a redistribution license or add these cached abstracts to the public source bundle. Users must check rights before sharing a portable record containing source text. Text offsets refer to stored plain text rendered from the returned markup; original abstract markup is retained in the source record. Retrieval is bounded, not exhaustive, and cached acquisition dates do not mean a fresh literature search. New source retraction flags exclude affected records in the current run without rewriting the installed snapshot. The software cannot guarantee complete or current retraction detection.

Only curated candidate names, separately entered public outcome phrases and explicitly supplied public PubMed identifiers are sent in this lookup. Free-text questions, product source wording, visit details, medicines and allergies are not included. Public-service failure or offline cache misses preserve local results with an explicit coverage warning; no records is not a clinical finding of no effect or safety.

## On-device medicine AI and clinical research additions (2026-11-12)

The pinned `sentence-transformers/all-MiniLM-L6-v2` sentence encoder and tokenizer (`data/models.lock.json`) are Apache-2.0. Self-hosted runtime assets retain `inference/MODEL-LICENSE.txt`; model/tokenizer hashes are checked before use. Group prototypes and synthetic test phrases were authored with AI coding assistance; they are not clinical annotations.

`data/demo/care_research_additions.json` adds a bounded selection of human studies/reviews to the public browser library. Only abstracts with CC BY/CC0 rights reported by Europe PMC are redistributed; unsupported or missing abstracts are omitted. Authors, article links, acquisition URLs and response SHA-256 are retained. Selection is not an exhaustive evidence review; a study formulation is not the actual bottle. Article-specific licence versions and attribution requirements remain authoritative at the original source. No full articles, images or figures are bundled.
